### The Generative Energy Arena (GEA): Incorporating Energy Awareness in Large Language Model (LLM) Human Evaluations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13302v1)
- **Authors**: Carlos Arriaga, Gonzalo Martínez, Eneko Sendin, Javier Conde, Pedro Reviriego
- **Abstract**: The evaluation of large language models is a complex task, in which several approaches have been proposed. The most common is the use of automated benchmarks in which LLMs have to answer multiple-choice questions of different topics. However, this method has certain limitations, being the most concerning, the poor correlation with the humans. An alternative approach, is to have humans evaluate the LLMs. This poses scalability issues as there is a large and growing number of models to evaluate making it impractical (and costly) to run traditional studies based on recruiting a number of evaluators and having them rank the responses of the models. An alternative approach is the use of public arenas, such as the popular LM arena, on which any user can freely evaluate models on any question and rank the responses of two models. The results are then elaborated into a model ranking. An increasingly important aspect of LLMs is their energy consumption and, therefore, evaluating how energy awareness influences the decisions of humans in selecting a model is of interest. In this paper, we present GEA, the Generative Energy Arena, an arena that incorporates information on the energy consumption of the model in the evaluation process. Preliminary results obtained with GEA are also presented, showing that for most questions, when users are aware of the energy consumption, they favor smaller and more energy efficient models. This suggests that for most user interactions, the extra cost and energy incurred by the more complex and top-performing models do not provide an increase in the perceived quality of the responses that justifies their use.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Generative Energy Arena (GEA), a platform designed to evaluate Large Language Models (LLMs) while incorporating information about their energy consumption during human evaluation. The GEA presents users with pairs of LLM responses to the same question, asks them to choose the better response, and then, if the user selects the response from the more energy-intensive model, asks if they would change their choice knowing the energy implications and assuming a slight loss of quality. The paper presents preliminary results from the GEA showing that users are significantly more likely to favor smaller, more energy-efficient models when made aware of the energy consumption differences. The authors argue that this demonstrates the importance of considering energy awareness in LLM evaluation and that, in many cases, the perceived quality difference between larger and smaller models may not justify the increased energy cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of incorporating energy awareness into human evaluation of LLMs is novel. Existing LLM evaluation benchmarks primarily focus on accuracy, fluency, or safety, neglecting the crucial dimension of energy efficiency. While the Colosseum project (mentioned but not fully operational or analyzed) had a similar intent, the GEA's focus on *understanding* the impact of energy information on human preferences, rather than just ranking models by efficiency, adds to its novelty. The two-step evaluation process, where users first evaluate based on quality and then consider energy, is a thoughtful approach to avoid biasing the initial judgment.

*   **Significance:** The work highlights a growing concern: the substantial environmental impact of LLMs. Demonstrating that users are willing to trade off some perceived quality for reduced energy consumption has significant implications for LLM development and deployment. It suggests that focusing solely on increasing model size and performance may not be the most sustainable path forward. The results suggest that there's a sweet spot where smaller, more efficient models can satisfy many user needs without sacrificing too much quality. If the observed trend holds true for a broader range of users and questions, it could drive a shift towards more energy-conscious LLM design.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses an important and often overlooked aspect of LLMs: energy consumption.
    *   The GEA's design is well-motivated and aims to minimize bias in user evaluations.
    *   The paper provides clear metrics for quantifying the impact of energy awareness.
    *   The preliminary results are compelling and suggest a significant effect of energy information on user choices.
    *   The paper identifies clear limitations and directions for future research, indicating a thorough understanding of the study's scope.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The sample size (694 questions) is relatively small and the user base is primarily MOOC students from a single university in Spain. This limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The study only compares models within the same family (e.g., GPT-4.1 variants). This design simplifies relative energy comparison but might not reflect real-world scenarios where users choose between models from different vendors.
    *   The "loss in quality" assumption in the prompt "Knowing that the other response consumes less energy, would you change your choice assuming a loss in quality?" may introduce some bias and could be explored using more neutral phrasing.
    *   The study is limited to the Spanish language, which could influence user preferences and perceptions of quality.
    *   The paper uses "energy consumption" as a proxy for actual energy use, since real data can vary based on numerous external factors.

*   **Potential Influence:** The GEA platform and the findings presented in this paper could encourage other researchers to investigate the role of energy awareness in LLM evaluation. It could also influence the design of LLM evaluation methodologies, leading to a more holistic assessment of model performance. The study may also prompt LLM developers to prioritize energy efficiency and provide users with information about the energy consumption of their models.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by bringing attention to the environmental impact of LLMs and by providing empirical evidence that users are receptive to considering energy consumption when evaluating models. However, the limitations regarding sample size, user demographics, language, and model comparison limit the broad applicability of the findings and the study's overall impact. The core idea is novel and timely, but more extensive research is needed to validate the results and to explore the nuances of energy awareness in LLM evaluation across different contexts. Given these considerations, I believe a score between 6 and 8 is appropriate. However, the weaknesses lead me to pick the lower range.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### FashionPose: Text to Pose to Relight Image Generation for Personalized Fashion Visualization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13311v1)
- **Authors**: Chuancheng Shi, Yixiang Chen, Burong Lei, Jichao Chen
- **Abstract**: Realistic and controllable garment visualization is critical for fashion e-commerce, where users expect personalized previews under diverse poses and lighting conditions. Existing methods often rely on predefined poses, limiting semantic flexibility and illumination adaptability. To address this, we introduce FashionPose, the first unified text-to-pose-to-relighting generation framework. Given a natural language description, our method first predicts a 2D human pose, then employs a diffusion model to generate high-fidelity person images, and finally applies a lightweight relighting module, all guided by the same textual input. By replacing explicit pose annotations with text-driven conditioning, FashionPose enables accurate pose alignment, faithful garment rendering, and flexible lighting control. Experiments demonstrate fine-grained pose synthesis and efficient, consistent relighting, providing a practical solution for personalized virtual fashion display.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper "FashionPose: Text to Pose to Relight Image Generation for Personalized Fashion Visualization" introduces a novel framework for generating personalized fashion images. The framework, called FashionPose, takes a natural language description as input and outputs a photorealistic image of a person wearing clothing described in the text, with controllable pose and lighting. This is achieved through a three-stage pipeline: (1) a CLIP-initialized transformer that translates the text into 2D human pose keypoints, (2) a diffusion model that synthesizes a pose-aligned person image conditioned on a reference image and the pose keypoints, and (3) a text-guided relighting module that adjusts the illumination of the image based on the input prompt. The authors also contribute a new dataset called PoseCap, which consists of natural language descriptions paired with 2D keypoints for training the pose generation component. Experiments are conducted to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework in terms of pose alignment, garment rendering, and flexible lighting control.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The novelty of this paper lies in the integration of text-to-pose generation, pose-guided image synthesis, and text-conditioned relighting into a unified framework. While each of these components has been explored in previous works, their combination, driven entirely by natural language, is a unique contribution. Specifically, the replacement of explicit pose annotations with text-driven conditioning enables a greater degree of semantic flexibility and control over both pose and lighting, which is a significant improvement over existing methods that rely on predefined or manually constructed poses. The PoseCap dataset is also a novel contribution, providing a valuable resource for training text-to-pose models.

However, it's important to note that the individual components of the pipeline build upon existing work. The CLIP-initialized transformer, the diffusion-based image synthesizer, and the relighting module leverage established techniques. The primary novelty is in the orchestration of these components within the FashionPose framework.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work stems from its potential to improve personalized fashion e-commerce. By allowing users to generate realistic previews of garments on models in various poses and lighting conditions using natural language descriptions, FashionPose can enhance the online shopping experience and potentially reduce returns. The fine-grained pose manipulation and flexible lighting adaptation capabilities also offer practical benefits for virtual fashion display.

However, the paper also acknowledges several limitations. The reliance on precise captions, the support for only global single-source illumination, and the potential for artifacts in multi-light scenes or with complex materials are important considerations. The paper also lacks a comparative study of its lighting performance against other relighting approaches. Furthermore, a more rigorous assessment of garment fidelity would strengthen the paper.

**Strengths:**

*   Unified framework for text-to-pose-to-relight image generation.
*   Text-driven conditioning for fine-grained pose manipulation and flexible lighting adaptation.
*   Introduction of the PoseCap dataset.
*   Clear presentation of the proposed method and experimental results.
*   Comprehensive qualitative results demonstrating improved pose alignment, garment rendering, and occlusion handling.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Reliance on precise captions, may not generalize well to more casual descriptions.
*   Limited support for complex illumination scenarios (multi-light scenes).
*   Lack of quantitative evaluation of relighting quality compared to other approaches.
*   Individual components leverage existing techniques, with primary novelty in the integration.
*   The paper could benefit from a deeper analysis of potential failure cases and robustness limitations.

**Potential Influence:**

The FashionPose framework has the potential to influence research in personalized fashion visualization, pose-guided image synthesis, and text-to-image generation. The PoseCap dataset can serve as a benchmark for future text-to-pose models. The text-driven conditioning approach can inspire new methods for controlling generative models with natural language.

**Score:**

Score: 7

**Rationale:**

The FashionPose paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of personalized fashion visualization by integrating existing techniques into a novel, unified framework. The text-driven approach to pose and lighting control offers a significant advantage over existing methods that rely on explicit pose annotations. The PoseCap dataset is also a welcome addition to the community. However, the reliance on precise captions, the limitations in handling complex illumination, and the lack of rigorous comparative evaluation against existing relighting methods limit the significance and scope of the paper. The novelty is primarily in the integration rather than individual component breakthroughs. Therefore, while the paper has potential to influence future research, a score of 7 reflects a reasonable balance between its strengths and weaknesses.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Revisiting Reliability in the Reasoning-based Pose Estimation Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13314v1)
- **Authors**: Junsu Kim, Naeun Kim, Jaeho Lee, Incheol Park, Dongyoon Han, Seungryul Baek
- **Abstract**: The reasoning-based pose estimation (RPE) benchmark has emerged as a widely adopted evaluation standard for pose-aware multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Despite its significance, we identified critical reproducibility and benchmark-quality issues that hinder fair and consistent quantitative evaluations. Most notably, the benchmark utilizes different image indices from those of the original 3DPW dataset, forcing researchers into tedious and error-prone manual matching processes to obtain accurate ground-truth (GT) annotations for quantitative metrics (\eg, MPJPE, PA-MPJPE). Furthermore, our analysis reveals several inherent benchmark-quality limitations, including significant image redundancy, scenario imbalance, overly simplistic poses, and ambiguous textual descriptions, collectively undermining reliable evaluations across diverse scenarios. To alleviate manual effort and enhance reproducibility, we carefully refined the GT annotations through meticulous visual matching and publicly release these refined annotations as an open-source resource, thereby promoting consistent quantitative evaluations and facilitating future advancements in human pose-aware multimodal reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Revisiting Reliability in the Reasoning-based Pose Estimation Benchmark":

**Summary:**

The paper identifies and addresses issues within the reasoning-based pose estimation (RPE) benchmark, a popular evaluation tool for pose-aware multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The authors highlight reproducibility problems stemming from the benchmark's use of different image indices compared to the original 3DPW dataset, forcing manual matching for quantitative evaluation. They also point out limitations in the benchmark's quality, including image redundancy, scenario imbalance, overly simplistic poses, and ambiguous textual descriptions. To address these problems, the authors carefully refined the ground truth annotations by visually matching each RPE image to its original 3DPW counterpart and made the refined annotations publicly available. Finally, quantitative experiments demonstrate the practical utility of the released refined annotations and their validity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main contribution is the identification and remediation of reproducibility and quality issues in a widely used benchmark. While not introducing a novel algorithm or model, this work addresses practical problems significantly hindering the progress of the field. The creation and release of refined ground truth annotations are a valuable resource. The novelty lies in the systematic analysis and correction of errors and limitations within an established evaluation framework rather than introducing fundamentally new methods.

*   **Significance:** The RPE benchmark has become important for evaluating pose-aware MLLMs. By improving its reliability, the authors make a significant contribution to fair and consistent evaluations. This will allow for more meaningful comparisons between different models and accelerate progress in this rapidly developing area. The open-sourcing of the corrected annotations is a crucial aspect, enabling other researchers to perform quantitative evaluations without the laborious and error-prone manual matching process.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear identification of specific issues (reproducibility, quality) with concrete examples.
    *   Rigorous process of refining ground truth annotations.
    *   Open-sourcing of the refined annotations, making a tangible contribution to the community.
    *   Demonstrates the impact of these fixes with quantitative results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The core contribution involves fixing existing problems rather than introducing completely novel concepts.
    *   The analysis, while insightful, does not delve into very sophisticated statistical methods for quantifying the benchmark weaknesses.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has a high potential for influence. By addressing practical problems and releasing a valuable resource, the authors are likely to impact how pose-aware MLLMs are evaluated and compared. This, in turn, will lead to more reliable research and faster progress in the field.
* **Justification:** The paper is valuable and will be actively used by the community. However, given that it focuses on correcting problems rather than presenting completely new research, a very high score wouldn't be fair. The authors address very important limitations and provide a significant resource. The study is well-executed and thorough in its correction strategy. Its contribution lies within the improvement of the community workflow and reproducibility which ultimately promotes robust research.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Imitation Game: Turing Machine Imitator is Length Generalizable Reasoner
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13332v1)
- **Authors**: Zhouqi Hua, Wenwei Zhang, Chengqi Lyu, Yuzhe Gu, Songyang Gao, Kuikun Liu, Kai Chen
- **Abstract**: Length generalization, the ability to solve problems of longer sequences than those observed during training, poses a core challenge of Transformer-based large language models (LLM). Although existing studies have predominantly focused on data-driven approaches for arithmetic operations and symbolic manipulation tasks, these approaches tend to be task-specific with limited overall performance. To pursue a more general solution, this paper focuses on a broader case of reasoning problems that are computable, i.e., problems that algorithms can solve, thus can be solved by the Turing Machine. From this perspective, this paper proposes Turing MAchine Imitation Learning (TAIL) to improve the length generalization ability of LLMs. TAIL synthesizes chain-of-thoughts (CoT) data that imitate the execution process of a Turing Machine by computer programs, which linearly expands the reasoning steps into atomic states to alleviate shortcut learning and explicit memory fetch mechanism to reduce the difficulties of dynamic and long-range data access in elementary operations. To validate the reliability and universality of TAIL, we construct a challenging synthetic dataset covering 8 classes of algorithms and 18 tasks. Without bells and whistles, TAIL significantly improves the length generalization ability as well as the performance of Qwen2.5-7B on various tasks using only synthetic data, surpassing previous methods and DeepSeek-R1. The experimental results reveal that the key concepts in the Turing Machine, instead of the thinking styles, are indispensable for TAIL for length generalization, through which the model exhibits read-and-write behaviors consistent with the properties of the Turing Machine in their attention layers. This work provides a promising direction for future research in the learning of LLM reasoning from synthetic data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Turing Machine Imitation Learning (TAIL), a novel data-driven approach designed to improve the length generalization capabilities of large language models (LLMs).  TAIL's core idea is to generate chain-of-thought (CoT) data that mimics the execution of a Turing Machine. This involves three key components: Linear Transition (ensuring a linear progression of reasoning steps to avoid shortcuts), Atomic States (breaking down reasoning into minimal units), and Memory Fetcher (explicitly retrieving necessary operand data at each step). The authors create a synthetic dataset across 18 tasks and 8 algorithmic categories to test TAIL.  Experiments show that fine-tuning LLMs (specifically Qwen2.5-7B) on this TAIL-generated data significantly improves length generalization compared to existing methods and even surpasses DeepSeek-R1. The paper also includes ablation studies to demonstrate the importance of each TAIL component and attention map visualizations to confirm the model learns Turing-Machine-like read-and-write behavior.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to length generalization. The core idea of leveraging the Turing Machine as a blueprint for structuring CoT data is original and well-motivated. While the idea of using abstract machines is not completely new, the concrete implementation within a CoT framework with the specific components of Linear Transition, Atomic States, and Memory Fetcher is a significant contribution. The synthetic dataset, encompassing a broader range of algorithmic problems than prior work, further strengthens the novelty.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a critical limitation of LLMs: their struggle with length generalization. This is a fundamental hurdle towards achieving more robust and reliable reasoning. The empirical results are impressive, demonstrating substantial improvements in length generalization across various tasks.  The fact that TAIL-trained models outperform previous methods and even a larger model like DeepSeek-R1 indicates the effectiveness of the approach. The ablation studies and attention map visualizations provide valuable insights into *why* TAIL works, which is crucial for advancing the field. The finding that minimalist CoT with TAIL principles is effective challenges the assumption that complex "thinking styles" are necessary for generalization. This has implications for how we design training data.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-motivated and clear problem statement:** The paper clearly articulates the length generalization problem and its importance.
*   **Novel approach:** TAIL is a conceptually elegant and practical solution with a strong theoretical basis.
*   **Comprehensive experiments:**  The diverse dataset and rigorous experimental evaluation provide compelling evidence for TAIL's effectiveness.
*   **Insightful analysis:**  The ablation studies and attention map visualizations deepen our understanding of the model's behavior.
*   **Strong results:** The paper demonstrates significant performance gains compared to existing approaches.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Synthetic Data Dependence:** TAIL relies on synthetic data. While this allows for controlled experiments and precise manipulation of the CoT structure, it raises questions about its applicability to more complex, real-world scenarios. It would be valuable to see how well TAIL transfers to tasks with noisy or ambiguous data.
*   **Limited Cross-Task Generalization:** The authors acknowledge the limited cross-task generalization within the same algorithm. This means that a model trained on one TAIL task might not perform well on another, even if they both belong to, say, Dynamic Programming. Overcoming this limitation is a key area for future research.
*   **CoT Length Expansion:** As highlighted by the authors, TAIL's reliance on explicit reasoning steps can lead to longer CoT sequences.  This increases computation and latency, potentially hindering its practicality for certain applications. Further research is needed to optimize the CoT length while maintaining generalization performance.
*   **Complexity limitations:** The method relies on flattening the computational process which might be hard when dealing with complex algorithmic structures such as infinite loops or probabilistic approaches.

**Potential Influence:**

TAIL has the potential to significantly influence research in the following areas:

*   **CoT Design:** The paper offers a new paradigm for designing CoT data based on mimicking the execution of a Turing Machine.
*   **Length Generalization:** TAIL provides a concrete and effective approach for improving length generalization in LLMs.
*   **Reasoning in LLMs:** The paper contributes to our understanding of how LLMs perform reasoning and how to guide them towards more reliable behavior.
*   **Synthetic Data Generation:** The paper demonstrates the power of synthetic data for training LLMs and provides a template for creating structured training data.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a strong contribution, but it is not without limitations. The novelty is high, and the experimental results are compelling. However, the reliance on synthetic data and the lack of strong cross-task generalization temper the overall impact. The approach could be potentially limited to certain types of tasks as well.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Survey of Context Engineering for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13334v1)
- **Authors**: Lingrui Mei, Jiayu Yao, Yuyao Ge, Yiwei Wang, Baolong Bi, Yujun Cai, Jiazhi Liu, Mingyu Li, Zhong-Zhi Li, Duzhen Zhang, Chenlin Zhou, Jiayi Mao, Tianze Xia, Jiafeng Guo, Shenghua Liu
- **Abstract**: The performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) is fundamentally determined by the contextual information provided during inference. This survey introduces Context Engineering, a formal discipline that transcends simple prompt design to encompass the systematic optimization of information payloads for LLMs. We present a comprehensive taxonomy decomposing Context Engineering into its foundational components and the sophisticated implementations that integrate them into intelligent systems. We first examine the foundational components: context retrieval and generation, context processing and context management. We then explore how these components are architecturally integrated to create sophisticated system implementations: retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), memory systems and tool-integrated reasoning, and multi-agent systems. Through this systematic analysis of over 1300 research papers, our survey not only establishes a technical roadmap for the field but also reveals a critical research gap: a fundamental asymmetry exists between model capabilities. While current models, augmented by advanced context engineering, demonstrate remarkable proficiency in understanding complex contexts, they exhibit pronounced limitations in generating equally sophisticated, long-form outputs. Addressing this gap is a defining priority for future research. Ultimately, this survey provides a unified framework for both researchers and engineers advancing context-aware AI.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "A Survey of Context Engineering for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

This paper introduces the concept of "Context Engineering" as a formal discipline for optimizing information payloads for Large Language Models (LLMs), going beyond simple prompt engineering. It presents a comprehensive taxonomy, categorizing Context Engineering into foundational "Components" (Context Retrieval and Generation, Context Processing, Context Management) and sophisticated "Implementations" (Retrieval-Augmented Generation, Memory Systems, Tool-Integrated Reasoning, Multi-Agent Systems).  The survey analyzes over 1400 research papers. It identifies a fundamental asymmetry: LLMs are proficient at *understanding* complex contexts due to context engineering, but are limited in their ability to *generate* equally sophisticated long-form outputs.  The paper proposes this gap as a key focus for future research and offers a unified framework for advancing context-aware AI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its formalization of "Context Engineering" as a distinct discipline and in the proposed taxonomy. While individual techniques within these categories have been explored extensively, framing them under a unified concept and a structured hierarchy provides a fresh perspective. However, the concepts themselves, like RAG, tool use, and prompt optimization, are already well-known.
*   **Significance:** The survey is significant for several reasons:
    *   **Organization:** It organizes a rapidly expanding and somewhat fragmented research area.
    *   **Clarity:** It clarifies the relationships between various techniques (e.g., linking RAG to external memory and prompt engineering to component orchestration).
    *   **Roadmap:** It provides a roadmap for researchers and engineers to navigate the complexities of context-aware AI.
    *   **Gap Identification:** The identification of the asymmetry between context comprehension and generation capabilities is a valuable contribution, highlighting a critical challenge for future research.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive scope:** The survey covers a broad range of relevant techniques and systems.
    *   **Structured taxonomy:** The classification into Components and Implementations is well-reasoned and helpful.
    *   **Clear writing:** The paper is well-written and relatively easy to understand.
    *   **Practical implications:** It offers insights for both researchers and practitioners.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited true "novelty" beyond synthesis:** Most individual techniques discussed are not new. The paper's strength is in its aggregation and organization.
    *   **Descriptive rather than deeply analytical:** While the paper provides a comprehensive overview, it lacks in-depth critical analyses and comparisons of different techniques within each category.
    *   **Future is limited:** The future research directions are already common in the field so it doesn't offer new direction.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper provides a valuable service to the field by synthesizing and organizing a large body of existing research on techniques related to using LLMs with external context. While the individual components discussed are not necessarily novel, the comprehensive taxonomy and the framing of "Context Engineering" as a distinct discipline are helpful contributions. However, the impact is somewhat limited by a lack of truly novel insights beyond aggregation and the description of future research that does not change the state of the art. It is a useful survey that is well organized, well written, with clear future directions, but it is somewhat limited in the novelty of its analysis and insights.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Comparing Apples to Oranges: A Dataset & Analysis of LLM Humour Understanding from Traditional Puns to Topical Jokes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13335v1)
- **Authors**: Tyler Loakman, William Thorne, Chenghua Lin
- **Abstract**: Humour, as a complex language form, is derived from myriad aspects of life, whilst existing work on computational humour has focussed almost exclusively on short pun-based jokes. In this work, we investigate whether the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to explain humour depends on the particular humour form. We compare models on simple puns and more complex topical humour that requires knowledge of real-world entities and events. In doing so, we curate a dataset of 600 jokes split across 4 joke types and manually write high-quality explanations. These jokes include heterographic and homographic puns, contemporary internet humour, and topical jokes, where understanding relies on reasoning beyond "common sense", rooted instead in world knowledge regarding news events and pop culture. Using this dataset, we compare the zero-shot abilities of a range of LLMs to accurately and comprehensively explain jokes of different types, identifying key research gaps in the task of humour explanation. We find that none of the tested models (inc. reasoning models) are capable of reliably generating adequate explanations of all joke types, further highlighting the narrow focus of most works in computational humour on overly simple joke forms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Comparing Apples to Oranges: A Dataset & Analysis of LLM Humour Understanding from Traditional Puns to Topical Jokes" addresses the limitations of existing computational humor research, which predominantly focuses on short, pun-based jokes. The authors argue that humor understanding requires more than just basic semantic and phonetic knowledge; it often relies on complex reasoning and knowledge retrieval of real-world events and pop culture phenomena. To investigate this, they create a novel, balanced dataset of 600 jokes, categorized into traditional puns (homographic and heterographic), non-topical Reddit jokes, and topical Reddit jokes. Each joke is paired with a high-quality, human-authored explanation. The authors then empirically assess the ability of several state-of-the-art LLMs to explain these jokes in a zero-shot setting, comparing their performance across different joke types. The study evaluates the LLM-generated explanations using both human evaluation and automatic metrics, including an LLM judge. A qualitative case study further explores the challenges of understanding complex topical humor.  The results indicate a significant performance gap in LLMs' ability to explain different joke types, with topical humor proving considerably more challenging than traditional puns.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel contributions:

*   **Dataset:** The creation of a balanced dataset that incorporates diverse joke types, moving beyond the common focus on puns, is a significant step. The inclusion of high-quality, human-authored explanations adds substantial value. The URLs provided for topical joke context is a useful added feature.
*   **Empirical Study:** The comprehensive evaluation of a range of LLMs on this dataset, using both human and automatic metrics, provides valuable insights into their capabilities and limitations in understanding different types of humor.
*   **Focus on Explanation:** Shifting the focus from simple humor detection to the more complex task of humor explanation is a welcome direction.

**Significance:** The paper's findings are important for several reasons:

*   **Highlighting Limitations:** It clearly demonstrates that current LLMs struggle with humor that requires real-world knowledge and reasoning, highlighting the limitations of models trained primarily on generic text data. This is critical for advancing the field.
*   **Guiding Future Research:** The dataset and analysis provide a solid foundation for future research on computational humor, particularly in developing models that can better handle complex and nuanced forms of humor.  By identifying the performance gaps, the work can guide the development of more specialized architectures and training strategies.
*   **Real-world Relevance:** Emphasizing complex topical humor is beneficial, since most humor in online and TV contexts falls within this domain.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Using 3 human evaluators and a separate model judge adds robustness.
*   **Transparent reporting:** The inclusion of Appendix elements (A.1-A.7) aids with reproduction.

**Strengths:**

*   Well-defined research questions and hypotheses.
*   Clearly described methodology and evaluation metrics.
*   Rigorous experimental setup with a comprehensive set of models.
*   Detailed analysis of results and insightful discussion.
*   The dataset has strong potential for future research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dataset Size:** While balanced, a dataset of 600 jokes is still relatively small. A larger dataset could provide more robust results.
*   **Reddit Specificity:** The reliance on Reddit jokes, even with the non-topical/topical separation, limits the generalizability of the findings somewhat. Reddit humor has a particular style and culture.
*   **Evaluation Subjectivity:** Despite using multiple evaluators, the evaluation of humor explanation remains inherently subjective. While agreement statistics were good, it is still a factor.  The LLM judge helps, but LLM judgement is not perfect.
*   **Lack of Generation:** There is no generation element to the paper. It would be interesting to see results concerning the dataset being used to train models to generate different types of jokes.
*   **Some limitations were not considered:** In addition to the limitations described in the paper, there are some other factors that could be included. Such limitations include the potential impact of cultural differences on humor understanding, which are not explicitly addressed in the paper; and, how the specific choices in dataset filtering influenced the type of jokes included.
*   **Lack of dataset use statement:** Even if the dataset is meant to be used for educational purposes only, a dataset use statement would prevent its potential misuse.

**Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of computational humor. Its focus on complex humor and the development of a novel dataset will encourage researchers to move beyond simple puns and develop more sophisticated models for understanding and generating humor. The rigorous evaluation methodology provides a valuable benchmark for future research.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper provides a valuable contribution to the field. However, there are also some considerable weaknesses that need to be considered when assigning an overall score. Overall, this is a very well-written paper.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Training Transformers with Enforced Lipschitz Constants
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13338v1)
- **Authors**: Laker Newhouse, R. Preston Hess, Franz Cesista, Andrii Zahorodnii, Jeremy Bernstein, Phillip Isola
- **Abstract**: Neural networks are often highly sensitive to input and weight perturbations. This sensitivity has been linked to pathologies such as vulnerability to adversarial examples, divergent training, and overfitting. To combat these problems, past research has looked at building neural networks entirely from Lipschitz components. However, these techniques have not matured to the point where researchers have trained a modern architecture such as a transformer with a Lipschitz certificate enforced beyond initialization. To explore this gap, we begin by developing and benchmarking novel, computationally-efficient tools for maintaining norm-constrained weight matrices. Applying these tools, we are able to train transformer models with Lipschitz bounds enforced throughout training. We find that optimizer dynamics matter: switching from AdamW to Muon improves standard methods -- weight decay and spectral normalization -- allowing models to reach equal performance with a lower Lipschitz bound. Inspired by Muon's update having a fixed spectral norm, we co-design a weight constraint method that improves the Lipschitz vs. performance tradeoff on MLPs and 2M parameter transformers. Our 2-Lipschitz transformer on Shakespeare text reaches validation accuracy 60%. Scaling to 145M parameters, our 10-Lipschitz transformer reaches 21% accuracy on internet text. However, to match the NanoGPT baseline validation accuracy of 39.4%, our Lipschitz upper bound increases to 10^264. Nonetheless, our Lipschitz transformers train without stability measures such as layer norm, QK norm, and logit tanh softcapping.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores training transformers with enforced Lipschitz bounds, aiming to improve stability, generalization, and robustness. The authors develop computationally efficient tools to maintain norm-constrained weight matrices and train transformer models with Lipschitz bounds enforced throughout training. They find that optimizer dynamics (switching from AdamW to Muon) and weight constraint methods significantly affect the Lipschitz vs. performance tradeoff. They introduce spectral soft cap and spectral hammer as weight constraint methods and demonstrate a <2-Lipschitz transformer on Shakespeare text reaching 60% validation accuracy. Larger models (145M parameters) are also trained with enforced Lipschitz bounds on internet text, though achieving competitive performance requires very high Lipschitz bounds.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in training transformer models *with Lipschitz constraints enforced throughout training,* which contrasts with previous work like LipsFormer that does not enforce constraints during training. The introduction of spectral soft cap and spectral hammer as weight regularization techniques, especially the connection to Muon's bounded update norm, also represents a novel contribution.
*   **Significance:** Enforcing Lipschitz bounds can potentially address several pressing issues in deep learning, including adversarial robustness, training stability, and generalization. The ability to train transformers without stability measures like layer norm, QK norm, and logit softcapping (while maintaining a Lipschitz bound) is significant. Further, the paper’s exploration of how different optimizers and constraint methods interact offers valuable insights for future research.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly defines the problem and presents a well-motivated approach.
    *   The introduction of spectral soft cap and spectral hammer is interesting, and the theoretical justification linking spectral soft cap to Muon's bounded update norm is solid.
    *   The empirical results, particularly the comparison of different optimizers and constraint methods, are comprehensive. The experiments on both MLPs and transformers across various datasets provide strong evidence for the claims.
    *   The negative results, such as needing extremely high Lipschitz bounds to match NanoGPT baseline accuracy, are valuable in highlighting the challenges of this approach.
    *   The authors are transparent about the limitations, such as hyperparameter tuning challenges, and point to future research directions.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The main limitation is the loose Lipschitz bound computed in Section 4. As described in the results, for larger models the computed Lipschitz bound becomes astronomical (10^264) in order to reach competitive performance. Though the authors test activation values and comment that they are far from the worst case, it limits the practical benefits of claimed Lipschitz constraint.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of *why* Muon consistently outperforms AdamW in this context. While the bounded update norm is mentioned, further investigation into the underlying dynamics would be valuable.
    *   The impact on training speed isn’t clearly elucidated. While the abstract mentions a "training speed benefit" in smaller settings, this benefit fades on the NanoGPT benchmark, and the overall implications are not entirely clear.
    *   More theoretical analysis linking their practical approach of applying constraint to weights in terms of guarantees for Lipschitzness would strengthen the claims.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work could influence future research on training more robust and stable transformers. The insights into optimizer dynamics and weight constraint methods might lead to new regularization techniques. The methods for enforcing Lipschitz bounds at train time will likely be influential for works in differential privacy and adversarial robustness in deep learning. The negative results, like the high Lipschitz bounds, can guide efforts towards more effective architectures or constraint techniques.

Score: 6

Justification:

The paper presents a valuable and novel approach to training transformers by enforcing Lipschitz bounds throughout training. The empirical results are strong, and the insights into optimizers and regularization techniques are noteworthy. The limitations relating to computed Lipschitz bounds prevents a higher score, but the general techniques for training are still useful. Overall, while the results do not offer a “drop-in” solution for stable, robust transformers, they represent a significant step forward and open new avenues for research.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Taming Diffusion Transformer for Real-Time Mobile Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13343v1)
- **Authors**: Yushu Wu, Yanyu Li, Anil Kag, Ivan Skorokhodov, Willi Menapace, Ke Ma, Arpit Sahni, Ju Hu, Aliaksandr Siarohin, Dhritiman Sagar, Yanzhi Wang, Sergey Tulyakov
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Transformers (DiT) have shown strong performance in video generation tasks, but their high computational cost makes them impractical for resource-constrained devices like smartphones, and real-time generation is even more challenging. In this work, we propose a series of novel optimizations to significantly accelerate video generation and enable real-time performance on mobile platforms. First, we employ a highly compressed variational autoencoder (VAE) to reduce the dimensionality of the input data without sacrificing visual quality. Second, we introduce a KD-guided, sensitivity-aware tri-level pruning strategy to shrink the model size to suit mobile platform while preserving critical performance characteristics. Third, we develop an adversarial step distillation technique tailored for DiT, which allows us to reduce the number of inference steps to four. Combined, these optimizations enable our model to achieve over 10 frames per second (FPS) generation on an iPhone 16 Pro Max, demonstrating the feasibility of real-time, high-quality video generation on mobile devices.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Taming Diffusion Transformer for Real-Time Mobile Video Generation" addresses the computational challenges of deploying Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) for video generation on resource-constrained mobile devices. It proposes a three-pronged optimization pipeline: (1) High-compression VAE to reduce latent space dimensionality, (2) a KD-guided sensitivity-aware tri-level pruning strategy to shrink the DiT model, and (3) adversarial step distillation to minimize inference steps. The combination enables real-time (over 10 FPS) high-quality video generation on an iPhone 16 Pro Max, demonstrating the feasibility of DiT-based video generation on mobile devices.  The key innovations lie in adapting and combining existing techniques tailored explicitly for DiTs on mobile, along with a novel adversarial distillation approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty resides in the specific combination and adaptation of existing techniques for DiT video generation on mobile, rather than groundbreaking theoretical advancements. The high-compression VAE, model pruning, and step distillation are all established concepts. However, the tailoring of a tri-level pruning method with KD guidance and a specially designed adversarial distillation specifically for DiTs are non-trivial contributions. The discriminator design with the initial K frozen DiT blocks is a novel component. The systematic investigation of the trade-off between VAE compression and quality for this specific task is also valuable.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is primarily in its practical impact. Enabling real-time, high-quality video generation on mobile devices opens a wide range of applications, from interactive content creation to augmented reality and personalized media experiences. While the individual components of the pipeline might not be entirely novel, their synergistic combination and rigorous engineering to achieve a specific performance target (real-time mobile generation) are significant achievements. The experiments on different VAE compression levels are well done and can guide future work. The paper demonstrates that DiTs, known for their computational intensity, can be tamed for practical applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive optimization pipeline addressing multiple bottlenecks.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating real-time performance on mobile hardware.
    *   Ablation studies highlighting the importance of different components.
    *   Detailed explanation of the pruning strategy and the discriminator architecture.
    *   The focus on mobile deployment makes the paper particularly valuable in the context of pervasive computing.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The core techniques (VAE compression, pruning, distillation) are not entirely new. The novelty lies primarily in their adaptation and integration.
    *   Some of the details regarding training and architectural choices, although stated to be standard practices, lack clarity in the main paper, which is a common issue of space limitation. A more comprehensive supplemental section could address that issue
    *   The comparison to previous methods could be strengthened. While VBench scores are provided, a more direct comparison (e.g., subjective A/B testing) with other mobile video generation techniques would be valuable. Also, there is a lack of reproducibility, as most state-of-the-art VDMs used for comparison are trained on internal datasets that cannot be fully disclosed or released.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has strong potential to influence the development of mobile video generation technologies. It provides a blueprint for optimizing DiTs for resource-constrained environments, paving the way for new mobile applications and user experiences. The carefully designed optimization pipeline could inspire more research that focus on pushing the boundaries of what can be done on mobile devices.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the critical points above, the paper demonstrates a significant engineering effort to adapt DiTs, a powerful but computationally intensive model, for real-time video generation on mobile devices. While the individual components are not entirely novel, their specific combination and tailoring, coupled with strong experimental results, warrant a relatively high score. The focus on real-time performance and mobile deployment makes this work practically relevant and likely to influence future research and development in this area.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffuman4D: 4D Consistent Human View Synthesis from Sparse-View Videos with Spatio-Temporal Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13344v1)
- **Authors**: Yudong Jin, Sida Peng, Xuan Wang, Tao Xie, Zhen Xu, Yifan Yang, Yujun Shen, Hujun Bao, Xiaowei Zhou
- **Abstract**: This paper addresses the challenge of high-fidelity view synthesis of humans with sparse-view videos as input. Previous methods solve the issue of insufficient observation by leveraging 4D diffusion models to generate videos at novel viewpoints. However, the generated videos from these models often lack spatio-temporal consistency, thus degrading view synthesis quality. In this paper, we propose a novel sliding iterative denoising process to enhance the spatio-temporal consistency of the 4D diffusion model. Specifically, we define a latent grid in which each latent encodes the image, camera pose, and human pose for a certain viewpoint and timestamp, then alternately denoising the latent grid along spatial and temporal dimensions with a sliding window, and finally decode the videos at target viewpoints from the corresponding denoised latents. Through the iterative sliding, information flows sufficiently across the latent grid, allowing the diffusion model to obtain a large receptive field and thus enhance the 4D consistency of the output, while making the GPU memory consumption affordable. The experiments on the DNA-Rendering and ActorsHQ datasets demonstrate that our method is able to synthesize high-quality and consistent novel-view videos and significantly outperforms the existing approaches. See our project page for interactive demos and video results: https://diffuman4d.github.io/ .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Diffuman4D paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Diffuman4D: 4D Consistent Human View Synthesis from Sparse-View Videos with Spatio-Temporal Diffusion Models" addresses the challenge of generating high-fidelity and consistent novel views of human performances from sparse-view video input.  The core innovation is a spatio-temporal diffusion model employing a sliding iterative denoising process. This process involves encoding input views and pose information as conditioning latents, creating a 4D latent grid, and then denoising this grid using a sliding window approach alternating between spatial and temporal dimensions.  A structural prior in the form of a 3D human skeleton sequence extracted from the sparse-view video is used to guide the generation process. Finally, the denoised latents are decoded into target view videos, which are used to reconstruct a 4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS) representation for real-time rendering. The authors demonstrate improved performance on DNA-Rendering and ActorsHQ datasets compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits good novelty in several aspects:

    *   **Sliding Iterative Denoising:**  The core contribution is the sliding iterative denoising scheme within a 4D diffusion model context.  This is not simply applying an existing sliding window technique; instead, they carefully integrate it into the diffusion denoising process with alternating spatial and temporal refinement. This is a key differentiator.
    *   **Skeleton-Conditioned Diffusion:** While using human pose priors isn't entirely novel, the way they integrate the 3D skeleton sequence into the diffusion model via a pixel-aligned feature (RGB map) representing the projected skeletons is a smart and effective conditioning approach.  The combination with Plucker coordinates for camera pose further strengthens the conditioning.
    *   **End-to-End Pipeline:** Combining the diffusion model with 4DGS reconstruction provides a complete pipeline for novel view synthesis and real-time rendering.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in:

    *   **Addressing Sparse-View Limitations:** Sparse-view 4D human reconstruction is a challenging problem, and the proposed method offers a substantial improvement over existing approaches, particularly those relying on direct reconstruction or generic diffusion models.
    *   **Improved Consistency:** The emphasis on spatio-temporal consistency is critical for realistic novel view synthesis, which is often a weakness of generative models. Their strategy tackles this problem head-on.
    *   **Practical Application:** Enabling real-time rendering via 4DGS enhances the practical utility of the method, making it applicable to augmented reality, virtual production, and other interactive applications.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and limitations of existing methods.
    *   **Well-Designed Methodology:** The proposed method is well-motivated, and the different components are logically integrated.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The quantitative and qualitative comparisons on DNA-Rendering and ActorsHQ clearly demonstrate the superiority of their approach. The ablation studies further validate the contributions of the sliding iterative denoising and skeleton conditioning.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors have processed and intend to release their processed version of the DNA-Rendering dataset, improving reproducibility in the community.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** While the sliding iterative denoising aims for efficiency, diffusion models are inherently computationally intensive.  Details on wall-clock training time and inference time *per view* would strengthen the presentation.
    *   **Reliance on External Tools:** The pipeline relies on pre-trained models like VAEs, skeleton estimators, and Colmap. While this is common, it can limit the overall performance and introduces potential error propagation. A discussion of these limitations is warranted.
    *   **Limited Novelty on 4DGS integration:** The paper employs Long Volcap, an existing 4DGS method, for the final reconstruction. A deeper integration with a new 4DGS technique could have strengthened the paper.
    *   **Generalization to Complex Interactions:** The paper acknowledges limitations with complex human-object interactions and new pose rendering. Expanding on this is important.
*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the fields of:

    *   **4D Human Reconstruction:** Provides a robust solution for sparse-view 4D human reconstruction, enabling more accessible capture setups.
    *   **Novel View Synthesis:** Demonstrates a powerful approach for generating consistent and realistic novel views of dynamic human performances.
    *   **Generative Modeling:** Offers insights into effectively conditioning diffusion models for spatio-temporal data.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**
The paper presents a solid and well-executed approach to a challenging problem in 4D human reconstruction. The sliding iterative denoising and skeleton-conditioned diffusion contribute meaningfully to improving consistency and quality in novel view synthesis from sparse views. While not revolutionary, the combination of techniques is smart, well-engineered, and leads to demonstrably better results than existing methods. The weaknesses mentioned above, especially regarding the reliance on pre-trained models and lack of detailed performance metrics, prevent it from achieving a higher score.  The paper will likely influence future research in this area and potentially find practical applications.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### VideoITG: Multimodal Video Understanding with Instructed Temporal Grounding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13353v1)
- **Authors**: Shihao Wang, Guo Chen, De-an Huang, Zhiqi Li, Minghan Li, Guilin Li, Jose M. Alvarez, Lei Zhang, Zhiding Yu
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have revealed that selecting informative and relevant video frames can significantly improve the performance of Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). Current methods, such as reducing inter-frame redundancy, employing separate models for image-text relevance assessment, or utilizing temporal video grounding for event localization, substantially adopt unsupervised learning paradigms, whereas they struggle to address the complex scenarios in long video understanding. We propose Instructed Temporal Grounding for Videos (VideoITG), featuring customized frame sampling aligned with user instructions. The core of VideoITG is the VidThinker pipeline, an automated annotation framework that explicitly mimics the human annotation process. First, it generates detailed clip-level captions conditioned on the instruction; then, it retrieves relevant video segments through instruction-guided reasoning; finally, it performs fine-grained frame selection to pinpoint the most informative visual evidence. Leveraging VidThinker, we construct the VideoITG-40K dataset, containing 40K videos and 500K instructed temporal grounding annotations. We then design a plug-and-play VideoITG model, which takes advantage of visual language alignment and reasoning capabilities of Video-LLMs, for effective frame selection in a discriminative manner. Coupled with Video-LLMs, VideoITG achieves consistent performance improvements across multiple multimodal video understanding benchmarks, showing its superiority and great potentials for video understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VIDEOITG: Multimodal Video Understanding with Instructed Temporal Grounding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VideoITG, a new approach to improve video understanding in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). The core idea is to use instruction-guided temporal grounding, which involves selecting video frames based on user instructions. The authors create VideoITG-40K, a large dataset with 40,000 videos and 500,000 instruction-guided annotations, using an automated annotation pipeline called VidThinker.  VidThinker mimics human annotation through three stages: clip captioning, clip retrieval, and fine-grained frame localization.  The paper also presents a VideoITG model, which can be plugged into existing Video-LLMs to improve frame selection. The model is evaluated on multiple benchmarks, demonstrating performance improvements over existing methods that use uniform frame sampling.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Positive:** The concept of instruction-guided temporal grounding is novel and addresses a significant limitation of existing Video-LLMs, which often rely on uniform frame sampling that misses key information. The automated annotation pipeline, VidThinker, is a valuable contribution, enabling the creation of a large-scale, high-quality dataset.
    *   **Negative:** While instruction-guided sampling in general grounding has been attempted. The approach of automating annotation is not revolutionary, the novelty lies more in the specific implementation and how it's tailored for video and integration with LLMs.
*   **Significance:**

    *   **Positive:** The paper shows tangible performance improvements on various video understanding benchmarks, which underscores its practical significance. By integrating VideoITG, the authors demonstrate that more intelligent frame selection can outperform simply scaling up model size. This has important implications for resource-efficient video understanding.
    *   **Negative:** The gains achieved may be incremental. While the performance increases are consistent, the percentage improvements might not be considered groundbreaking in all cases, although they are substantial in several benchmarks. The true impact hinges on how well this generalizes to truly unseen, diverse datasets and tasks.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset:** The creation and release of the VideoITG-40K dataset are a major strength. The dataset will likely serve as a valuable resource for the research community.
    *   **Methodology:** The VidThinker pipeline is well-designed and motivated by a human-centric annotation process.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The consistent performance gains across multiple benchmarks are compelling.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Existing LLMs:** The VideoITG model is essentially a plug-in, meaning its performance is heavily dependent on the capabilities of the underlying Video-LLM.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't fully discuss the computational overhead of using VideoITG. While it improves performance, it adds an additional step in the processing pipeline, increasing the compute cost.
    *   **Generalizability:** While the paper provides results on standard benchmarks, there could be limitations on the generalizability of the instruction categories.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Encouraging more research on instruction-guided video understanding.
    *   Providing a valuable dataset for training and evaluating Video-LLMs.
    *   Demonstrating the importance of intelligent frame selection in video understanding.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by introducing instruction-guided temporal grounding and providing a comprehensive dataset for this task. The idea is elegant and well-motivated, and the experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness. The design of VidThinker is particularly impressive, showing a clear line of thought towards mimicking human reasoning and annotation. However, the dependence on existing LLMs and the incremental nature of some performance gains prevent it from being a truly transformative work. Considering the novel approach and substantial dataset, as well as its practical relevance, the paper warrants a high but non-perfect score.

**Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10
